def construct_points(N):
    # permutation sigma(i) = 2*i mod N (1-indexed)
    sigma = {}
    for i in range(1,N+1):
        val = (2*i) % N
        if val == 0:
            val = N
        sigma[i]=val
    points = []
    for i in range(1,N+1):
        points.append((i,i))
        if sigma[i]!=i:
            points.append((i, sigma[i]))
    return points, sigma

def check_no_isosceles(points):
    # brute force check all quadruples for small N
    pts = points
    n = len(pts)
    for i in range(n):
        x1,y1 = pts[i]
        for j in range(i+1,n):
            x2,y2 = pts[j]
            # check if same row
            if y1==y2:
                # consider as top base candidate
                for k in range(n):
                    if k==i or k==j: continue
                    x3,y3 = pts[k]
                    if y3==y1: continue
                    for l in range(k+1,n):
                        if l==i or l==j: continue
                        x4,y4 = pts[l]
                        if y4!=y3: continue
                        # now we have two rows y1 and y3
                        # check isosceles trapezoid condition
                        # need bases parallel to x-axis: rows are y1 and y3.
                        # ensure four distinct points
                        # Check condition |x1 - x3| == |x2 - x4| or |x1 - x4| == |x2 - x3|
                        if abs(x1 - x3) == abs(x2 - x4) or abs(x1 - x4) == abs(x2 - x3):
                            return False
            # similarly for same column
            if x1==x2:
                for k in range(n):
                    if k==i or k==j: continue
                    x3,y3 = pts[k]
                    if x3==x1: continue
                    for l in range(k+1,n):
                        if l==i or l==j: continue
                        x4,y4 = pts[l]
                        if x4!=x3: continue
                        if abs(y1 - y3) == abs(y2 - y4) or abs(y1 - y4) == abs(y2 - y3):
                            return False
    return True

for N in [5,7,11]:
    pts,_ = construct_points(N)
    ok = check_no_isosceles(pts)
    print(N, len(pts), ok)

[CODE] (turn 13):
def construct_points(N):
    # permutation sigma(i) = 2*i mod N (1-indexed)
    sigma = {}
    for i in range(1,N+1):
        val = (2*i) % N
        if val == 0:
            val = N
        sigma[i]=val
    points = []
    for i in range(1,N+1):
        points.append((i,i))
        if sigma[i]!=i:
            points.append((i, sigma[i]))
    return points, sigma

def check_no_isosceles(points):
    # brute force check all quadruples for small N
    pts = points
    n = len(pts)
    for i in range(n):
        x1,y1 = pts[i]
        for j in range(i+1,n):
            x2,y2 = pts[j]
            # check if same row
            if y1==y2:
                # consider as top base candidate
                for k in range(n):
                    if k==i or k==j: continue
                    x3,y3 = pts[k]
                    if y3==y1: continue
                    for l in range(k+1,n):
                        if l==i or l==j: continue
                        x4,y4 = pts[l]
                        if y4!=y3: continue
                        # now we have two rows y1 and y3
                        # check isosceles trapezoid condition
                        # need bases parallel to x-axis: rows are y1 and y3.
                        # ensure four distinct points
                        # Check condition |x1 - x3| == |x2 - x4| or |x1 - x4| == |x2 - x3|
                        if abs(x1 - x3) == abs(x2 - x4) or abs(x1 - x4) == abs(x2 - x3):
                            return False
            # similarly for same column
            if x1==x2:
                for k in range(n):
                    if k==i or k==j: continue
                    x3,y3 = pts[k]
                    if x3==x1: continue
                    for l in range(k+1,n):
                        if l==i or l==j: continue
                        x4,y4 = pts[l]
                        if x4!=x3: continue
                        if abs(y1 - y3) == abs(y2 - y4) or abs(y1 - y4) == abs(y2 - y3):
                            return False
    return True

for N in [5,7,11]:

    ok = check_no_isosceles(pts)

def find_counterexample(N):
    pts,_ = construct_points(N)
    n=len(pts)
    for i in range(n):
        x1,y1=pts[i]
        for j in range(i+1,n):
            x2,y2=pts[j]
            if y1==y2:
                for k in range(n):
                    if k in (i,j): continue
                    x3,y3=pts[k]
                    if y3==y1: continue
                    for l in range(k+1,n):
                        if l in (i,j): continue
                        x4,y4=pts[l]
                        if y4!=y3: continue
                        if abs(x1-x3)==abs(x2-x4) or abs(x1-x4)==abs(x2-x3):
                            return (pts[i],pts[j],pts[k],pts[l])
    return None

print(find_counterexample(5))

[CODE] (turn 15):
def perm_diff_unique(N):
    perm = {}
    for i in range(1,N+1):
        val = (i + (i-1)) % N
        if val == 0:
            val = N
        perm[i]=val
    # compute absolute diffs
    diffs = [abs(perm[i]-i) for i in range(1,N+1)]
    return perm, diffs

for N in [5,7,9,11]:
    perm,diffs = perm_diff_unique(N)
    print(N, diffs)

def perm_diff_unique(N):
    perm = {}
    for i in range(1,N+1):
        val = (i + (i-1)) % N
        if val == 0:
            val = N
        perm[i]=val
    # compute absolute diffs
    diffs = [abs(perm[i]-i) for i in range(1,N+1)]
    return perm, diffs

for N in [5,7,9,11]:

[CODE] (turn 16):
import itertools
import numpy as np
from scipy.optimize import milp, LinearConstraint, Bounds

def max_points_N(N):
    n_vars = N*N
    c = -np.ones(n_vars)  # maximize sum
    # constraints list
    A = []
    lb = []
    ub = []
    # For each pair of rows
    for r1 in range(N):
        for r2 in range(r1+1,N):
            # enumerate all unordered pairs of columns for each row
            cols = list(range(N))
            pairs = list(itertools.combinations(cols,2))
            for a,b in pairs:
                for ccol,d in pairs:
                    if (a+b == ccol+d) or (abs(a-b) == abs(ccol-d)):
                        # forbid all four points
                        coeff = np.zeros(n_vars)
                        coeff[r1*N + a] = 1
                        coeff[r1*N + b] = 1
                        coeff[r2*N + ccol] = 1
                        coeff[r2*N + d] = 1
                        A.append(coeff)
                        lb.append(-np.inf)
                        ub.append(3)
    # similarly for columns (swap roles)
    for col1 in range(N):
        for col2 in range(col1+1,N):
            rows = list(range(N))
            pairs = list(itertools.combinations(rows,2))
            for a,b in pairs:
                for c,d in pairs:
                    if (a+b == c+d) or (abs(a-b) == abs(c-d)):
                        coeff = np.zeros(n_vars)
                        coeff[a*N + col1] = 1
                        coeff[b*N + col1] = 1
                        coeff[c*N + col2] = 1
                        coeff[d*N + col2] = 1
                        A.append(coeff)
                        lb.append(-np.inf)
                        ub.append(3)
    if not A:
        lincon = None
    else:
        A = np.vstack(A)
        lb = np.array(lb)
        ub = np.array(ub)
        lincon = LinearConstraint(A, lb, ub)
    bounds = Bounds(0,1)
    integrality = np.ones(n_vars, dtype=int)
    res = milp(c=c, constraints=lincon, bounds=bounds, integrality=integrality, options={'disp': False})
    if not res.success:
        print("Failed", res.message)
        return None
    total = int(round(-res.fun))  # since we minimized negative sum
    return total

for N in [3,4,5]:
    print(N, max_points_N(N))

import numpy as np
from scipy.optimize import milp, LinearConstraint, Bounds

def max_points_N(N):
    n_vars = N*N
    c = -np.ones(n_vars)  # maximize sum
    # constraints list

    ub = []

                        coeff[r2*N + d] = 1
                        A.append(coeff)
                        lb.append(-np.inf)
                        ub.append(3)
    # similarly for columns (swap roles)
    for col1 in range(N):
        for col2 in range(col1+1,N):
            rows = list(range(N))
            pairs = list(itertools.combinations(rows,2))
            for a,b in pairs:
                for c,d in pairs:

                        coeff = np.zeros(n_vars)
                        coeff[a*N + col1] = 1
                        coeff[b*N + col1] = 1

                        A.append(coeff)
                        lb.append(-np.inf)
                        ub.append(3)

        ub = np.array(ub)

        print("Failed", res.message)

from math import comb
def max_points(N):
    import numpy as np
    from scipy.optimize import milp, LinearConstraint, Bounds
    # variable ordering: first N*N x_{i,j} (flatten row-major), then N*(2N-1) z_row[i,s], then N*(2N-1) z_col[j,s]
    num_x = N*N
    sums = list(range(2, 2*N+1))
    S = len(sums)
    num_zrow = N*S
    num_zcol = N*S
    total_vars = num_x + num_zrow + num_zcol
    # objective: maximize sum of x
    c = np.zeros(total_vars)
    c[:num_x] = -1  # minimize negative to maximize
    # constraints list
    A = []
    lb = []
    ub = []
    # For each row i and sum s, for each pair a<b with a+b=s, enforce z >= x_a + x_b -1
    # We'll index x_{i,j} as i*N + (j-1) where i,j from 1..N
    for i in range(N):
        for s_idx, s in enumerate(sums):
            # find pairs (a,b) with a<b and a+b=s, both in 1..N
            pairs = [(a,b) for a in range(1,N+1) for b in range(a+1,N+1) if a+b==s]
            for a,b in pairs:
                row = np.zeros(total_vars)
                # x_i_a
                row[i*N + (a-1)] = 1
                # x_i_b
                row[i*N + (b-1)] = 1
                # -zrow
                row[num_x + i*S + s_idx] = -1
                A.append(row)
                lb.append(-1)  # x_a + x_b -1 - z >= 0 => row >= 0? Actually constraint: x_a + x_b -1 <= z => x_a + x_b -1 - z <= 0
                ub.append(0)
    # For each column j and sum s, similar constraints using x_{i,j}
    for j in range(N):
        for s_idx, s in enumerate(sums):
            pairs = [(a,b) for a in range(1,N+1) for b in range(a+1,N+1) if a+b==s]
            for a,b in pairs:
                # rows a,b correspond to y coordinates? Wait column constraints involve rows (y) indices.
                # For column j, we consider points (a,j) and (b,j) where a,b are y values (rows)
                row = np.zeros(total_vars)
                row[(a-1)*N + j] = 1
                row[(b-1)*N + j] = 1
                row[num_x + num_zrow + j*S + s_idx] = -1
                A.append(row)
                lb.append(-1)
                ub.append(0)
    # Row sum uniqueness: sum_i zrow[i,s] <= 1
    for s_idx in range(S):
        row = np.zeros(total_vars)
        row[num_x:num_x+num_zrow] = 0
        for i in range(N):
            row[num_x + i*S + s_idx] = 1
        A.append(row)
        lb.append(-np.inf)
        ub.append(1)
    # Column sum uniqueness: sum_j zcol[j,s] <= 1
    for s_idx in range(S):
        row = np.zeros(total_vars)
        for j in range(N):
            row[num_x + num_zrow + j*S + s_idx] = 1
        A.append(row)
        lb.append(-np.inf)
        ub.append(1)
    # variable bounds: x binary, z binary (0/1)
    bounds = Bounds(lb=np.zeros(total_vars), ub=np.ones(total_vars))
    # integrality: all variables integer
    integrality = np.ones(total_vars)
    # Build LinearConstraint
    A = np.array(A)
    lc = LinearConstraint(A, lb, ub)
    res = milp(c, constraints=lc, bounds=bounds, integrality=integrality, options={'disp': False})
    if not res.success:
        print("failed")
        return None
    M = -res.fun
    return int(round(M))

for N in [3,4,5]:
    print(N, max_points(N))

from math import comb
def max_points(N):
    import numpy as np
    from scipy.optimize import milp, LinearConstraint, Bounds
    # variable ordering: first N*N x_{i,j} (flatten row-major), then N*(2N-1) z_row[i,s], then N*(2N-1) z_col[j,s]
    num_x = N*N
    sums = list(range(2, 2*N+1))
    S = len(sums)
    num_zrow = N*S
    num_zcol = N*S
    total_vars = num_x + num_zrow + num_zcol
    # objective: maximize sum of x
    c = np.zeros(total_vars)
    c[:num_x] = -1  # minimize negative to maximize
    # constraints list
    A = []
    lb = []
    ub = []
    # For each row i and sum s, for each pair a<b with a+b=s, enforce z >= x_a + x_b -1
    # We'll index x_{i,j} as i*N + (j-1) where i,j from 1..N
    for i in range(N):
        for s_idx, s in enumerate(sums):
            # find pairs (a,b) with a<b and a+b=s, both in 1..N
            pairs = [(a,b) for a in range(1,N+1) for b in range(a+1,N+1) if a+b==s]
            for a,b in pairs:
                row = np.zeros(total_vars)
                # x_i_a
                row[i*N + (a-1)] = 1
                # x_i_b
                row[i*N + (b-1)] = 1
                # -zrow
                row[num_x + i*S + s_idx] = -1
                A.append(row)
                lb.append(-1)  # x_a + x_b -1 - z >= 0 => row >= 0? Actually constraint: x_a + x_b -1 <= z => x_a + x_b -1 - z <= 0
                ub.append(0)
    # For each column j and sum s, similar constraints using x_{i,j}
    for j in range(N):
        for s_idx, s in enumerate(sums):
            pairs = [(a,b) for a in range(1,N+1) for b in range(a+1,N+1) if a+b==s]
            for a,b in pairs:
                # rows a,b correspond to y coordinates? Wait column constraints involve rows (y) indices.
                # For column j, we consider points (a,j) and (b,j) where a,b are y values (rows)
                row = np.zeros(total_vars)
                row[(a-1)*N + j] = 1
                row[(b-1)*N + j] = 1
                row[num_x + num_zrow + j*S + s_idx] = -1
                A.append(row)
                lb.append(-1)
                ub.append(0)
    # Row sum uniqueness: sum_i zrow[i,s] <= 1
    for s_idx in range(S):
        row = np.zeros(total_vars)
        row[num_x:num_x+num_zrow] = 0
        for i in range(N):
            row[num_x + i*S + s_idx] = 1
        A.append(row)
        lb.append(-np.inf)
        ub.append(1)
    # Column sum uniqueness: sum_j zcol[j,s] <= 1
    for s_idx in range(S):
        row = np.zeros(total_vars)
        for j in range(N):
            row[num_x + num_zrow + j*S + s_idx] = 1
        A.append(row)
        lb.append(-np.inf)
        ub.append(1)
    # variable bounds: x binary, z binary (0/1)
    bounds = Bounds(lb=np.zeros(total_vars), ub=np.ones(total_vars))
    # integrality: all variables integer
    integrality = np.ones(total_vars)
    # Build LinearConstraint
    A = np.array(A)
    lc = LinearConstraint(A, lb, ub)
    res = milp(c, constraints=lc, bounds=bounds, integrality=integrality, options={'disp': False})
    if not res.success:
        print("failed")
        return None
    M = -res.fun
    return int(round(M))

for N in [3,4,5]:
    print(N, max_points(N))

[CODE] (turn 8):
def is_valid(selection, N):
    # selection is set of (x,y) tuples
    # check condition for isosceles trapezoid with horizontal base
    # For each pair of rows y1<y2, collect x's in each row
    rows = {}
    cols = {}
    for x,y in selection:
        rows.setdefault(y, []).append(x)
        cols.setdefault(x, []).append(y)
    # check rows
    for y1, xs1 in rows.items():
        if len(xs1) < 2: continue
        for y2, xs2 in rows.items():
            if y2 <= y1 or len(xs2) < 2: continue
            # compute all sums of unordered pairs in each row
            sums1 = set(a+b for a,b in itertools.combinations(xs1,2))
            sums2 = set(a+b for a,b in itertools.combinations(xs2,2))
            if sums1 & sums2:
                return False
    # check columns similarly
    for x1, ys1 in cols.items():
        if len(ys1) < 2: continue
        for x2, ys2 in cols.items():
            if x2 <= x1 or len(ys2) < 2: continue
            sums1 = set(a+b for a,b in itertools.combinations(ys1,2))
            sums2 = set(a+b for a,b in itertools.combinations(ys2,2))
            if sums1 & sums2:
                return False
    return True

    best=None

        # iterate subsets of size r? Too many.
        pass

[CODE] (turn 9):
def max_points_bruteforce(N):
    points = [(x,y) for x in range(1,N+1) for y in range(1,N+1)]
    maxsize=0
    best=set()
    for mask in range(1<<len(points)):
        sel = {points[i] for i in range(len(points)) if mask>>i &1}
        if is_valid(sel,N):
            if len(sel)>maxsize:
                maxsize=len(sel)
                best=sel
    return maxsize, best

print(max_points_bruteforce(3))

    best=set()
    for mask in range(1<<len(points)):
        sel = {points[i] for i in range(len(points)) if mask>>i &1}
        if is_valid(sel,N):
            if len(sel)>maxsize:
                maxsize=len(sel)
                best=sel
    return maxsize, best

[CODE] (turn 10):
def construct(N, K, rows_to_upgrade):
    # base edges: (i,i) and (i,i+1) mod N (1-indexed)
    edges=set()
    for i in range(1,N+1):
        edges.add((i,i))
        edges.add((i, ((i)%N)+1))  # i+1 with wrap
    # add extra edges
    for i in rows_to_upgrade:
        j = ((i+K-1)%N)+1
        edges.add((i,j))
    return edges

def is_valid_set(edges,N):
    return is_valid(edges,N)

def test(N):
    # try K=2
    K=2
    # try upgrading floor((N-1)/2) rows
    rows_to_upgrade = list(range(1, (N-1)//2 +1))
    edges=construct(N,K,rows_to_upgrade)
    print("size", len(edges), "valid?", is_valid_set(edges,N))
    # try all subsets of rows of size floor((N-1)/2) maybe

def find_violation(edges,N):
    rows={}
    cols={}
    for x,y in edges:
        rows.setdefault(y, []).append(x)
        cols.setdefault(x, []).append(y)
    # check rows
    for y1, xs1 in rows.items():
        if len(xs1)<2: continue
        for y2, xs2 in rows.items():
            if y2<=y1 or len(xs2)<2: continue
            sums1=set(a+b for a,b in itertools.combinations(xs1,2))
            sums2=set(a+b for a,b in itertools.combinations(xs2,2))
            inter=sums1 & sums2
            if inter:
                return ("row", y1, y2, inter)
    for x1, ys1 in cols.items():
        if len(ys1)<2: continue
        for x2, ys2 in cols.items():
            if x2<=x1 or len(ys2)<2: continue
            sums1=set(a+b for a,b in itertools.combinations(ys1,2))
            sums2=set(a+b for a,b in itertools.combinations(ys2,2))
            inter=sums1 & sums2
            if inter:
                return ("col", x1, x2, inter)
    return None

[CODE] (turn 15):
def max_points_backtrack(N):
    # order points maybe by row then column
    points = [(x,y) for y in range(1,N+1) for x in range(1,N+1)]
    # maintain selected set, row dict, col dict, used sums rows, used sums cols
    best = 0
    best_set = set()
    # Precompute pairs for each row/col for sums
    from collections import defaultdict
    def dfs(idx, selected, rows, cols, used_row_sums, used_col_sums):
        nonlocal best, best_set
        # prune: maximum possible remaining points = total points - idx + len(selected)
        if len(selected) + (len(points)-idx) <= best:
            return
        if idx == len(points):
            if len(selected) > best:
                best = len(selected)
                best_set = selected.copy()
            return
        x,y = points[idx]
        # option 1: skip
        dfs(idx+1, selected, rows, cols, used_row_sums, used_col_sums)
        # option 2: try to add point
        # compute new sums that would be created in row y and column x
        # row y currently has columns rows[y] (list)
        new_row_sums = []
        conflict=False
        for x2 in rows.get(y, []):
            s = x + x2
            if s in used_row_sums:
                conflict=True
                break
            new_row_sums.append(s)
        if conflict:
            return
        # column x
        new_col_sums = []
        for y2 in cols.get(x, []):
            s = y + y2
            if s in used_col_sums:
                conflict=True
                break
            new_col_sums.append(s)
        if conflict:
            return
        # also need to ensure that within same row/col we don't create duplicate sums (but they are distinct because x distinct)
        # add point
        selected.add((x,y))
        rows.setdefault(y, []).append(x)
        cols.setdefault(x, []).append(y)
        # update used sums
        for s in new_row_sums:
            used_row_sums.add(s)
        for s in new_col_sums:
            used_col_sums.add(s)
        dfs(idx+1, selected, rows, cols, used_row_sums, used_col_sums)
        # backtrack
        selected.remove((x,y))
        rows[y].pop()
        if not rows[y]:
            del rows[y]
        cols[x].pop()
        if not cols[x]:
            del cols[x]
        for s in new_row_sums:
            used_row_sums.remove(s)
        for s in new_col_sums:
            used_col_sums.remove(s)
    dfs(0, set(), {}, {}, set(), set())
    return best, best_set

print(max_points_backtrack(5)[0])

import itertools

def construct(N):
    points=set()
    for i in range(1,N+1):
        j=i
        points.add((j,i))
        j2=i+1 if i<N else 1
        points.add((j2,i))
    return points

def has_iso_trap(points,N):
    # check horizontal bases
    rows={}
    for (x,y) in points:
        rows.setdefault(y, []).append(x)
    for y1,y2 in itertools.combinations(rows.keys(),2):
        xs1=rows[y1]
        xs2=rows[y2]
        # need two points in each row
        if len(xs1)<2 or len(xs2)<2:
            continue
        # check all unordered pairs
        for a,b in itertools.combinations(xs1,2):
            for c,d in itertools.combinations(xs2,2):
                if a+b==c+d:
                    # found isosceles trapezoid with horizontal bases
                    return True
    # vertical bases
    cols={}
    for (x,y) in points:
        cols.setdefault(x, []).append(y)
    for x1,x2 in itertools.combinations(cols.keys(),2):
        ys1=cols[x1]
        ys2=cols[x2]
        if len(ys1)<2 or len(ys2)<2:
            continue
        for a,b in itertools.combinations(ys1,2):
            for c,d in itertools.combinations(ys2,2):
                if a+b==c+d:
                    return True
    return False

for N in range(2,11):
    pts=construct(N)
    print(N, len(pts), has_iso_trap(pts,N))

def has_iso_trap(points,N):
    # check horizontal bases
    rows={}
    for (x,y) in points:
        rows.setdefault(y, []).append(x)
    for y1,y2 in itertools.combinations(rows.keys(),2):
        xs1=rows[y1]
        xs2=rows[y2]
        # need two points in each row
        if len(xs1)<2 or len(xs2)<2:
            continue
        # check all unordered pairs
        for a,b in itertools.combinations(xs1,2):
            for c,d in itertools.combinations(xs2,2):
                if a+b==c+d:
                    # found isosceles trapezoid with horizontal bases
                    return True
    # vertical bases
    cols={}
    for (x,y) in points:

        ys1=cols[x1]

        if len(ys1)<2 or len(ys2)<2:

    return False

def max_M(N):
    S=2*N-1
    # brute force search for max M satisfying inequality derived
    maxM=0
    for M in range(N, 2*N+1000): # up to maybe 3N
        if M*(M-N) <= 2*N*S:
            maxM=M
    return maxM

[CODE] (turn 7):
def max_points_N(N):
    # indices
    points = [(i+1,j+1) for i in range(N) for j in range(N)]  # (x,y)
    P = len(points)
    # variable order: first P x variables, then row-sum variables a_{y,s}, then col-sum variables b_{x,s}
    # mapping
    idx_x = {pt: i for i,pt in enumerate(points)}
    # sums range
    sums = list(range(2, 2*N+1))
    # row a variables
    a_vars = {}
    for y in range(1,N+1):
        for s in sums:
            a_vars[(y,s)] = None
    # column b variables
    b_vars = {}
    for x in range(1,N+1):
        for s in sums:
            b_vars[(x,s)] = None
    total_vars = P + len(a_vars) + len(b_vars)
    # Build constraints matrices for milp
    # We'll use scipy.optimize milp which expects linear constraints in form A_ub @ x <= b_ub, A_eq @ x = b_eq, bounds.
    # We'll accumulate constraints as list of rows.
    rows = []
    rhs = []
    senses = []  # 'le' or 'ge' etc; but milp uses A_ub and A_eq only. So we need to split.
    # We'll treat >= constraints by multiplying -1 to convert to <=.
    # lower bound constraints: a_{y,s} >= x_i + x_j - 1  => -a_{y,s} + x_i + x_j <= 1
    # similarly for b.
    # Build variable index mapping
    var_index = {}
    cur = 0
    for pt in points:
        var_index[('x',pt)] = cur
        cur += 1
    for y in range(1,N+1):
        for s in sums:
            var_index[('a',y,s)] = cur
            cur += 1
    for x in range(1,N+1):
        for s in sums:
            var_index[('b',x,s)] = cur
            cur += 1
    assert cur == total_vars
    # constraints for a lower bound
    for y in range(1,N+1):
        for s in sums:
            # find all pairs (i,j) with i<j, i+j = s, i,j in 1..N
            pairs = [(i,j) for i in range(1,N+1) for j in range(i+1,N+1) if i+j == s]
            if not pairs:
                continue
            for (i,j) in pairs:
                row = np.zeros(total_vars, dtype=int)
                row[var_index[('a',y,s)]] = -1
                row[var_index[('x',(i,y)]] = 1
                row[var_index[('x',(j,y)]] = 1
                rows.append(row)
                rhs.append(1)
    # constraints for b lower bound
    for x in range(1,N+1):
        for s in sums:
            pairs = [(i,j) for i in range(1,N+1) for j in range(i+1,N+1) if i+j == s]
            if not pairs:
                continue
            for (i,j) in pairs:
                row = np.zeros(total_vars, dtype=int)
                row[var_index[('b',x,s)]] = -1
                row[var_index[('x',(x,i)]] = 1
                row[var_index[('x',(x,j)]] = 1
                rows.append(row)
                rhs.append(1)
    # constraints sum_y a_{y,s} <= 1
    for s in sums:
        row = np.zeros(total_vars, dtype=int)
        for y in range(1,N+1):
            row[var_index[('a',y,s)]] = 1
        rows.append(row)
        rhs.append(1)
    # constraints sum_x b_{x,s} <= 1
    for s in sums:
        row = np.zeros(total_vars, dtype=int)
        for x in range(1,N+1):
            row[var_index[('b',x,s)]] = 1
        rows.append(row)
        rhs.append(1)
    # Build A_ub matrix
    A_ub = np.vstack(rows)
    b_ub = np.array(rhs, dtype=int)
    # bounds: x variables binary, a,b binary (0/1)
    bounds = [(0,1)]*total_vars
    # objective: maximize sum of x variables
    c = np.zeros(total_vars)
    for pt in points:
        c[var_index[('x',pt)]] = -1  # milp minimizes c^T x, so use -1 to maximize
    # Use milp
    res = opt.milp(c=c, bounds=bounds, constraints=opt.LinearConstraint(A_ub, -np.inf, b_ub))
    if not res.success:
        print("MILP failed", res.message)
        return None
    x_sol = res.x
    total = int(round(-res.fun))  # since objective minimized negative sum
    return total

print(max_points_N(3))

[CODE] (turn 7):
def max_points_N(N):
    # indices
    points = [(i+1,j+1) for i in range(N) for j in range(N)]  # (x,y)
    P = len(points)
    # variable order: first P x variables, then row-sum variables a_{y,s}, then col-sum variables b_{x,s}
    # mapping
    idx_x = {pt: i for i,pt in enumerate(points)}
    # sums range
    sums = list(range(2, 2*N+1))
    # row a variables
    a_vars = {}
    for y in range(1,N+1):
        for s in sums:
            a_vars[(y,s)] = None
    # column b variables
    b_vars = {}
    for x in range(1,N+1):
        for s in sums:
            b_vars[(x,s)] = None
    total_vars = P + len(a_vars) + len(b_vars)
    # Build constraints matrices for milp
    # We'll use scipy.optimize milp which expects linear constraints in form A_ub @ x <= b_ub, A_eq @ x = b_eq, bounds.
    # We'll accumulate constraints as list of rows.
    rows = []
    rhs = []
    senses = []  # 'le' or 'ge' etc; but milp uses A_ub and A_eq only. So we need to split.
    # We'll treat >= constraints by multiplying -1 to convert to <=.
    # lower bound constraints: a_{y,s} >= x_i + x_j - 1  => -a_{y,s} + x_i + x_j <= 1
    # similarly for b.
    # Build variable index mapping
    var_index = {}
    cur = 0
    for pt in points:
        var_index[('x',pt)] = cur
        cur += 1
    for y in range(1,N+1):
        for s in sums:
            var_index[('a',y,s)] = cur
            cur += 1
    for x in range(1,N+1):
        for s in sums:
            var_index[('b',x,s)] = cur
            cur += 1
    assert cur == total_vars
    # constraints for a lower bound
    for y in range(1,N+1):
        for s in sums:
            # find all pairs (i,j) with i<j, i+j = s, i,j in 1..N
            pairs = [(i,j) for i in range(1,N+1) for j in range(i+1,N+1) if i+j == s]
            if not pairs:
                continue
            for (i,j) in pairs:
                row = np.zeros(total_vars, dtype=int)
                row[var_index[('a',y,s)]] = -1
                row[var_index[('x',(i,y)]] = 1
                row[var_index[('x',(j,y)]] = 1
                rows.append(row)
                rhs.append(1)
    # constraints for b lower bound
    for x in range(1,N+1):
        for s in sums:
            pairs = [(i,j) for i in range(1,N+1) for j in range(i+1,N+1) if i+j == s]
            if not pairs:
                continue
            for (i,j) in pairs:
                row = np.zeros(total_vars, dtype=int)
                row[var_index[('b',x,s)]] = -1
                row[var_index[('x',(x,i)]] = 1
                row[var_index[('x',(x,j)]] = 1
                rows.append(row)
                rhs.append(1)
    # constraints sum_y a_{y,s} <= 1
    for s in sums:
        row = np.zeros(total_vars, dtype=int)
        for y in range(1,N+1):
            row[var_index[('a',y,s)]] = 1
        rows.append(row)
        rhs.append(1)
    # constraints sum_x b_{x,s} <= 1
    for s in sums:
        row = np.zeros(total_vars, dtype=int)
        for x in range(1,N+1):
            row[var_index[('b',x,s)]] = 1
        rows.append(row)
        rhs.append(1)
    # Build A_ub matrix
    A_ub = np.vstack(rows)
    b_ub = np.array(rhs, dtype=int)
    # bounds: x variables binary, a,b binary (0/1)
    bounds = [(0,1)]*total_vars
    # objective: maximize sum of x variables
    c = np.zeros(total_vars)
    for pt in points:
        c[var_index[('x',pt)]] = -1  # milp minimizes c^T x, so use -1 to maximize
    # Use milp
    res = opt.milp(c=c, bounds=bounds, constraints=opt.LinearConstraint(A_ub, -np.inf, b_ub))
    if not res.success:
        print("MILP failed", res.message)
        return None
    x_sol = res.x
    total = int(round(-res.fun))  # since objective minimized negative sum
    return total

print(max_points_N(3))

def try_milp(N):
    import numpy as np, scipy.optimize as opt, itertools, math, sys, time
    points = [(i+1,j+1) for i in range(N) for j in range(N)]
    P = len(points)
    sums = list(range(2,2*N+1))
    var_index={}
    cur=0
    for pt in points:
        var_index[('x',pt)]=cur;cur+=1
    for y in range(1,N+1):
        for s in sums:
            var_index[('a',y,s)]=cur;cur+=1
    for x in range(1,N+1):
        for s in sums:
            var_index[('b',x,s)]=cur;cur+=1
    total_vars=cur
    rows=[]
    rhs=[]
    # a constraints
    for y in range(1,N+1):
        for s in sums:
            pairs=[(i,j) for i in range(1,N+1) for j in range(i+1,N+1) if i+j==s]
            for (i,j) in pairs:
                row=np.zeros(total_vars)
                row[var_index[('a',y,s)]]=-1
                row[var_index[('x',(i,y))]]=1
                row[var_index[('x',(j,y))]]=1
                rows.append(row);rhs.append(1)
    # b constraints
    for x in range(1,N+1):
        for s in sums:
            pairs=[(i,j) for i in range(1,N+1) for j in range(i+1,N+1) if i+j==s]
            for (i,j) in pairs:
                row=np.zeros(total_vars)
                row[var_index[('b',x,s)]]=-1
                row[var_index[('x',(x,i))]]=1
                row[var_index[('x',(x,j))]]=1
                rows.append(row);rhs.append(1)
    # sum a <=1
    for s in sums:
        row=np.zeros(total_vars)
        for y in range(1,N+1):
            row[var_index[('a',y,s)]]=1
        rows.append(row);rhs.append(1)
    # sum b <=1
    for s in sums:
        row=np.zeros(total_vars)
        for x in range(1,N+1):
            row[var_index[('b',x,s)]]=1
        rows.append(row);rhs.append(1)
    A_ub=np.vstack(rows)
    b_ub=np.array(rhs)
    bounds=[(0,1)]*total_vars
    c=np.zeros(total_vars)
    for pt in points:
        c[var_index[('x',pt)]]=-1
    try:
        res=opt.milp(c=c,bounds=bounds,constraints=opt.LinearConstraint(A_ub,-np.inf,b_ub))
        print("status",res.success)
        print(res.message)
        print("obj",res.fun)
    except Exception as e:
        print("exception",e)

def max_points_N(N):
    import numpy as np, scipy.optimize as opt, itertools, math
    points = [(i+1,j+1) for i in range(N) for j in range(N)]
    sums = list(range(2,2*N+1))
    var_index={}
    cur=0
    for pt in points:
        var_index[('x',pt)]=cur;cur+=1
    for y in range(1,N+1):
        for s in sums:
            var_index[('a',y,s)]=cur;cur+=1
    for x in range(1,N+1):
        for s in sums:
            var_index[('b',x,s)]=cur;cur+=1
    total_vars=cur
    rows=[]
    rhs=[]
    # a lower bound constraints
    for y in range(1,N+1):
        for s in sums:
            pairs=[(i,j) for i in range(1,N+1) for j in range(i+1,N+1) if i+j==s]
            for (i,j) in pairs:
                row=np.zeros(total_vars)
                row[var_index[('a',y,s)]]=-1
                row[var_index[('x',(i,y))]]=1
                row[var_index[('x',(j,y))]]=1
                rows.append(row);rhs.append(1)
    # b lower bound constraints
    for x in range(1,N+1):
        for s in sums:
            pairs=[(i,j) for i in range(1,N+1) for j in range(i+1,N+1) if i+j==s]
            for (i,j) in pairs:
                row=np.zeros(total_vars)
                row[var_index[('b',x,s)]]=-1
                row[var_index[('x',(x,i))]]=1
                row[var_index[('x',(x,j))]]=1
                rows.append(row);rhs.append(1)
    # sum a <=1
    for s in sums:
        row=np.zeros(total_vars)
        for y in range(1,N+1):
            row[var_index[('a',y,s)]]=1
        rows.append(row);rhs.append(1)
    # sum b <=1
    for s in sums:
        row=np.zeros(total_vars)
        for x in range(1,N+1):
            row[var_index[('b',x,s)]]=1
        rows.append(row);rhs.append(1)
    A_ub=np.vstack(rows)
    b_ub=np.array(rhs)
    bounds = opt.Bounds(0,1)  # same for all variables; will be broadcast?
    # Actually Bounds expects arrays of same shape.
    lower = np.zeros(total_vars)
    upper = np.ones(total_vars)
    bounds = opt.Bounds(lower, upper)
    c = np.zeros(total_vars)
    for pt in points:
        c[var_index[('x',pt)]] = -1
    res = opt.milp(c=c, bounds=bounds, constraints=opt.LinearConstraint(A_ub, -np.inf, b_ub))
    if not res.success:
        print("MILP failed:", res.message)
        return None
    total = int(round(-res.fun))
    return total

def get_solution(N):
    import numpy as np, scipy.optimize as opt, itertools, math
    points = [(i+1,j+1) for i in range(N) for j in range(N)]
    sums = list(range(2,2*N+1))
    var_index={}
    cur=0
    for pt in points:
        var_index[('x',pt)]=cur;cur+=1
    for y in range(1,N+1):
        for s in sums:
            var_index[('a',y,s)]=cur;cur+=1
    for x in range(1,N+1):
        for s in sums:
            var_index[('b',x,s)]=cur;cur+=1
    total_vars=cur
    rows=[]
    rhs=[]
    # a constraints
    for y in range(1,N+1):
        for s in sums:
            pairs=[(i,j) for i in range(1,N+1) for j in range(i+1,N+1) if i+j==s]
            for (i,j) in pairs:
                row=np.zeros(total_vars)
                row[var_index[('a',y,s)]]=-1
                row[var_index[('x',(i,y))]]=1
                row[var_index[('x',(j,y))]]=1
                rows.append(row);rhs.append(1)
    # b constraints
    for x in range(1,N+1):
        for s in sums:
            pairs=[(i,j) for i in range(1,N+1) for j in range(i+1,N+1) if i+j==s]
            for (i,j) in pairs:
                row=np.zeros(total_vars)
                row[var_index[('b',x,s)]]=-1
                row[var_index[('x',(x,i))]]=1
                row[var_index[('x',(x,j))]]=1
                rows.append(row);rhs.append(1)
    # sum a <=1
    for s in sums:
        row=np.zeros(total_vars)
        for y in range(1,N+1):
            row[var_index[('a',y,s)]]=1
        rows.append(row);rhs.append(1)
    # sum b <=1
    for s in sums:
        row=np.zeros(total_vars)
        for x in range(1,N+1):
            row[var_index[('b',x,s)]]=1
        rows.append(row);rhs.append(1)
    A_ub=np.vstack(rows)
    b_ub=np.array(rhs)
    lower=np.zeros(total_vars)
    upper=np.ones(total_vars)
    bounds=opt.Bounds(lower,upper)
    c=np.zeros(total_vars)
    for pt in points:
        c[var_index[('x',pt)]]=-1
    res=opt.milp(c=c,bounds=bounds,constraints=opt.LinearConstraint(A_ub,-np.inf,b_ub))
    sel=[]
    for pt in points:
        if res.x[var_index[('x',pt)]]>0.5:
            sel.append(pt)
    return sel, res

def max_points_N_int(N):
    import numpy as np, scipy.optimize as opt, itertools, math
    points = [(i+1,j+1) for i in range(N) for j in range(N)]
    sums = list(range(2,2*N+1))
    var_index={}
    cur=0
    for pt in points:
        var_index[('x',pt)]=cur;cur+=1
    for y in range(1,N+1):
        for s in sums:
            var_index[('a',y,s)]=cur;cur+=1
    for x in range(1,N+1):
        for s in sums:
            var_index[('b',x,s)]=cur;cur+=1
    total_vars=cur
    rows=[]
    rhs=[]
    # a lower bound constraints
    for y in range(1,N+1):
        for s in sums:
            pairs=[(i,j) for i in range(1,N+1) for j in range(i+1,N+1) if i+j==s]
            for (i,j) in pairs:
                row=np.zeros(total_vars)
                row[var_index[('a',y,s)]]=-1
                row[var_index[('x',(i,y))]]=1
                row[var_index[('x',(j,y))]]=1
                rows.append(row);rhs.append(1)
    # b lower bound constraints
    for x in range(1,N+1):
        for s in sums:
            pairs=[(i,j) for i in range(1,N+1) for j in range(i+1,N+1) if i+j==s]
            for (i,j) in pairs:
                row=np.zeros(total_vars)
                row[var_index[('b',x,s)]]=-1
                row[var_index[('x',(x,i))]]=1
                row[var_index[('x',(x,j))]]=1
                rows.append(row);rhs.append(1)
    # sum a <=1
    for s in sums:
        row=np.zeros(total_vars)
        for y in range(1,N+1):
            row[var_index[('a',y,s)]]=1
        rows.append(row);rhs.append(1)
    # sum b <=1
    for s in sums:
        row=np.zeros(total_vars)
        for x in range(1,N+1):
            row[var_index[('b',x,s)]]=1
        rows.append(row);rhs.append(1)
    A_ub=np.vstack(rows)
    b_ub=np.array(rhs)
    lower=np.zeros(total_vars)
    upper=np.ones(total_vars)
    bounds=opt.Bounds(lower,upper)
    c=np.zeros(total_vars)
    for pt in points:
        c[var_index[('x',pt)]]=-1
    integrality=np.zeros(total_vars, dtype=int)
    # all variables binary (0/1)
    integrality[:] = 1
    res=opt.milp(c=c,bounds=bounds, constraints=opt.LinearConstraint(A_ub,-np.inf,b_ub), integrality=integrality)
    if not res.success:
        print("MILP failed", res.message)
        return None
    total=int(round(-res.fun))
    return total

print(max_points_N_int(3))

def get_solution_int(N):
    import numpy as np, scipy.optimize as opt, itertools, math
    points = [(i+1,j+1) for i in range(N) for j in range(N)]
    sums = list(range(2,2*N+1))
    var_index={}
    cur=0
    for pt in points:
        var_index[('x',pt)]=cur;cur+=1
    for y in range(1,N+1):
        for s in sums:
            var_index[('a',y,s)]=cur;cur+=1
    for x in range(1,N+1):
        for s in sums:
            var_index[('b',x,s)]=cur;cur+=1
    total_vars=cur
    rows=[]
    rhs=[]
    # a constraints
    for y in range(1,N+1):
        for s in sums:
            pairs=[(i,j) for i in range(1,N+1) for j in range(i+1,N+1) if i+j==s]
            for (i,j) in pairs:
                row=np.zeros(total_vars)
                row[var_index[('a',y,s)]]=-1
                row[var_index[('x',(i,y))]]=1
                row[var_index[('x',(j,y))]]=1
                rows.append(row);rhs.append(1)
    # b constraints
    for x in range(1,N+1):
        for s in sums:
            pairs=[(i,j) for i in range(1,N+1) for j in range(i+1,N+1) if i+j==s]
            for (i,j) in pairs:
                row=np.zeros(total_vars)
                row[var_index[('b',x,s)]]=-1
                row[var_index[('x',(x,i))]]=1
                row[var_index[('x',(x,j))]]=1
                rows.append(row);rhs.append(1)
    # sum a <=1
    for s in sums:
        row=np.zeros(total_vars)
        for y in range(1,N+1):
            row[var_index[('a',y,s)]]=1
        rows.append(row);rhs.append(1)
    # sum b <=1
    for s in sums:
        row=np.zeros(total_vars)
        for x in range(1,N+1):
            row[var_index[('b',x,s)]]=1
        rows.append(row);rhs.append(1)
    A_ub=np.vstack(rows)
    b_ub=np.array(rhs)
    lower=np.zeros(total_vars)
    upper=np.ones(total_vars)
    bounds=opt.Bounds(lower,upper)
    c=np.zeros(total_vars)
    for pt in points:
        c[var_index[('x',pt)]]=-1
    integrality=np.ones(total_vars, dtype=int)
    res=opt.milp(c=c,bounds=bounds, constraints=opt.LinearConstraint(A_ub,-np.inf,b_ub), integrality=integrality)
    sel=[]
    for pt in points:
        if res.x[var_index[('x',pt)]]>0.5:
            sel.append(pt)
    return sel, res

[CODE] (turn 20):
def max_M_even_distribution(N):
    S = 2*N - 1
    maxM = 0
    for M in range(N, N*N+1):
        q, r = divmod(M, N)
        # compute sum C
        sumC = r * ( (q+1)*q //2 ) + (N - r) * ( q*(q-1)//2 )
        if sumC <= S:
            maxM = M
    return maxM

for N in range(2,11):
    print(N, max_M_even_distribution(N))

So total sums = N + 2m = N + N -1 = 2N -1 = S, exactly using all sums. So we would use all possible sums across rows, which is feasible.

Similarly for columns.

Thus we need to construct a 2-regular+ extra edges configuration where row sum sets partition the set of sums {2,...,2N} into N- r rows using 1 sum each (odd perhaps) and r rows using 3 sums each (mix of even and odd). Similarly for columns.

This seems plausible for large N.

We need to produce explicit construction for odd N. Let's attempt to construct such a set for general odd N.

Let N = 2m+1 (odd). Then r = m (since (N-1)/2 = m). So we need m rows of degree 3, m columns of degree 3, and the rest (m+1 rows) degree 2? Wait N rows total = 2m+1. Number of rows degree 3 = m, number degree 2 = N - m = m+1. Similarly columns.

(odd, odd, odd) => all sums even (since odd+odd=even). (even, even, odd) => a+b even, a+c odd, b+c odd => one even, two odd. (even, odd, odd) => a+b odd, a+c odd, b+c even => two odd, one even. So we can have either 2 odd +1 even, or 1 odd+2 even, or all even. We need to allocate sums such that each row's odd sums are unique across rows and total odd sums used equals N (since all odd sums must be used). Thus each degree-3 row will have exactly one odd sum and two even sums. That's feasible.

[CODE] (turn 1):
def check(n,k):
    sums_row = []
    for i in range(1,n+1):
        a = i
        b = i + k
        if b>n:
            b -= n
        sums_row.append(a+b)
    # check duplicates
    dup = len(sums_row) != len(set(sums_row))
    # column sums
    sums_col = []
    for j in range(1,n+1):
        r1 = j
        r2 = j - k
        if r2<1:
            r2 += n
        sums_col.append(r1+r2)
    dup_col = len(sums_col)!=len(set(sums_col))
    return dup, dup_col

n=9973
for k in range(1,n):
    dup, dupc = check(n,k)
    if not dup and not dupc:
        print("found",k)
        break

def check(n,k):
    sums_row = []
    for i in range(1,n+1):

        b = i + k
        if b>n:
            b -= n
        sums_row.append(a+b)
    # check duplicates
    dup = len(sums_row) != len(set(sums_row))
    # column sums
    sums_col = []
    for j in range(1,n+1):
        r1 = j
        r2 = j - k
        if r2<1:
            r2 += n
        sums_col.append(r1+r2)
    dup_col = len(sums_col)!=len(set(sums_col))
    return dup, dup_col

[CODE] (turn 2):
def construct(n):
    points=set()
    for i in range(1,n+1):
        a=i
        b=i+1
        if b>n:
            b-=n
        points.add((i,a))
        points.add((i,b))
    return points

def is_isosceles_trapezoid(p1,p2,p3,p4):
    # check if bases are horizontal (same y) or vertical (same x)
    # Determine if four points form a trapezoid with parallel bases (horizontal or vertical) and legs equal.
    # We'll check both orientations.
    pts=[p1,p2,p3,p4]
    # Check horizontal bases: need two points with same y (top) and two with same y (bottom), y1!=y2
    # and the four points distinct.
    # We'll try all partitions of points into two pairs with equal y.
    import itertools
    for (a,b,c,d) in itertools.permutations(pts,4):
        # treat a,b as top base (same y), c,d as bottom base (same y)
        if a[1]==b[1] and c[1]==d[1] and a[1]!=c[1]:
            # check legs: distances a-c and b-d equal (or a-d and b-c)
            # compute squared distances
            def sqdist(p,q):
                return (p[0]-q[0])**2+(p[1]-q[1])**2
            if sqdist(a,c)==sqdist(b,d):
                # also need that points are ordered correctly? Not needed.
                return True
            if sqdist(a,d)==sqdist(b,c):
                return True
    # vertical bases similarly
    for (a,b,c,d) in itertools.permutations(pts,4):
        if a[0]==b[0] and c[0]==d[0] and a[0]!=c[0]:
            def sqdist(p,q):
                return (p[0]-q[0])**2+(p[1]-q[1])**2
            if sqdist(a,c)==sqdist(b,d):
                return True
            if sqdist(a,d)==sqdist(b,c):
                return True
    return False

def check_no_iso(n):
    pts=construct(n)
    import itertools
    for quad in itertools.combinations(pts,4):
        if is_isosceles_trapezoid(*quad):
            return False, quad
    return True, None

for n in range(2,10):
    ok,_=check_no_iso(n)
    print(n, ok)

[CODE] (turn 2):
def construct(n):
    points=set()
    for i in range(1,n+1):
        a=i
        b=i+1
        if b>n:
            b-=n
        points.add((i,a))
        points.add((i,b))
    return points

def is_isosceles_trapezoid(p1,p2,p3,p4):
    # check if bases are horizontal (same y) or vertical (same x)
    # Determine if four points form a trapezoid with parallel bases (horizontal or vertical) and legs equal.
    # We'll check both orientations.
    pts=[p1,p2,p3,p4]
    # Check horizontal bases: need two points with same y (top) and two with same y (bottom), y1!=y2
    # and the four points distinct.
    # We'll try all partitions of points into two pairs with equal y.
    import itertools
    for (a,b,c,d) in itertools.permutations(pts,4):
        # treat a,b as top base (same y), c,d as bottom base (same y)
        if a[1]==b[1] and c[1]==d[1] and a[1]!=c[1]:
            # check legs: distances a-c and b-d equal (or a-d and b-c)
            # compute squared distances
            def sqdist(p,q):
                return (p[0]-q[0])**2+(p[1]-q[1])**2
            if sqdist(a,c)==sqdist(b,d):
                # also need that points are ordered correctly? Not needed.
                return True
            if sqdist(a,d)==sqdist(b,c):
                return True
    # vertical bases similarly
    for (a,b,c,d) in itertools.permutations(pts,4):
        if a[0]==b[0] and c[0]==d[0] and a[0]!=c[0]:
            def sqdist(p,q):
                return (p[0]-q[0])**2+(p[1]-q[1])**2
            if sqdist(a,c)==sqdist(b,d):
                return True
            if sqdist(a,d)==sqdist(b,c):
                return True
    return False

def check_no_iso(n):
    pts=construct(n)
    import itertools
    for quad in itertools.combinations(pts,4):
        if is_isosceles_trapezoid(*quad):
            return False, quad
    return True, None

for n in range(2,10):
    ok,_=check_no_iso(n)
    print(n, ok)

def check_midpoints(n):
    pts=construct(n)
    # rows
    row_mid={}
    for y in range(1,n+1):
        xs=[x for (r,x) in pts if r==y]
        if len(xs)>=2:
            mids=set()
            for i in range(len(xs)):
                for j in range(i+1,len(xs)):
                    mids.add((xs[i]+xs[j],2)) # store numerator, denominator 2
            row_mid[y]=mids
    # check disjoint
    rows=list(row_mid.keys())
    for i in range(len(rows)):
        for j in range(i+1,len(rows)):
            if row_mid[rows[i]] & row_mid[rows[j]]:
                return False, (rows[i],rows[j])
    # columns
    col_mid={}
    for x in range(1,n+1):
        ys=[y for (y,c) in pts if c==x]
        if len(ys)>=2:
            mids=set()
            for i in range(len(ys)):
                for j in range(i+1,len(ys)):
                    mids.add((ys[i]+ys[j],2))
            col_mid[x]=mids
    cols=list(col_mid.keys())
    for i in range(len(cols)):
        for j in range(i+1,len(cols)):
            if col_mid[cols[i]] & col_mid[cols[j]]:
                return False, (cols[i],cols[j])
    return True, None

for n in range(3,15):
    ok,_=check_midpoints(n)
    print(n, ok)

import itertools
from collections import defaultdict

def max_N_bruteforce(n):
    # Use ILP via MILP from scipy.optimize.milp if available
    import numpy as np, math, itertools
    from scipy.optimize import milp, LinearConstraint, Bounds
    # variables x_{i,j} binary for each cell
    Nvar = n*n
    # create variable index mapping
    idx = {}
    cnt=0
    for i in range(n):
        for j in range(n):
            idx[(i,j)] = cnt
            cnt+=1
    # objective: maximize sum x
    c = np.ones(Nvar, dtype=int)
    # constraints: for each row i, for each possible sum s (i.e., column pair sum)
    # we need that for each row i, for each sum s, at most one pair of selected points in that row has that sum.
    # This is complex to encode linearly.
    # Instead we can encode directly the condition that for any two rows i1,i2, the sets of midpoints are disjoint.
    # That's quadratic; not easy for MILP.
    return None

    return None

[CODE] (turn 6):
import itertools

def max_points_bruteforce_n(n):
    points = [(i,j) for i in range(1,n+1) for j in range(1,n+1)]
    best=0
    best_set=None
    # Use recursion row by row
    used_row_sums = set()
    used_col_sums = set()
    col_points = {j: [] for j in range(1,n+1)}
    def recurse(row, selected):
        nonlocal best,best_set,used_row_sums,used_col_sums
        if row>n:
            # all rows processed, compute total points
            total=len(selected)
            if total>best:
                best=total
                best_set=selected.copy()
            return
        # generate all subsets of columns for this row
        cols = list(range(1,n+1))
        # we can prune: max possible remaining points = (n-row+1)*n (if all columns selected)
        # but we need to respect column constraints later; ignore for now.
        for k in range(0,n+1):
            for subset in itertools.combinations(cols,k):
                # compute row sums for this subset
                row_sums=set()
                ok=True
                # compute pairwise sums
                for a,b in itertools.combinations(subset,2):
                    s=a+b
                    if s in used_row_sums:
                        ok=False
                        break
                    row_sums.add(s)
                if not ok:
                    continue
                # check column sums with existing points
                col_sums_added=[]
                for col in subset:
                    # add point (row,col)
                    # compute column sums with existing points in this column
                    for other_row in col_points[col]:
                        t=row+other_row
                        if t in used_col_sums:
                            ok=False
                            break
                        col_sums_added.append(t)
                    if not ok:
                        break
                if not ok:
                    continue
                # update structures
                # add row sums to used
                used_row_sums.update(row_sums)
                # add column sums to used
                used_col_sums.update(col_sums_added)
                # add points to col_points
                for col in subset:
                    col_points[col].append(row)
                selected.extend([(row,col) for col in subset])
                # recurse
                recurse(row+1, selected)
                # backtrack
                for _ in subset:
                    selected.pop()
                for col in subset:
                    col_points[col].pop()
                for s in row_sums:
                    used_row_sums.remove(s)
                for t in col_sums_added:
                    used_col_sums.remove(t)
        # also consider empty subset (k=0) already covered
    recurse(1, [])
    return best, best_set

best, best_set = max_points_bruteforce_n(5)
print(best)

import itertools

def max_subset_no_iso_trapezoid(n):
    points = [(i,j) for i in range(n) for j in range(n)]
    N = len(points)
    max_size = 0
    best = None
    # enumerate subsets via bitmask
    for mask in range(1<<N):
size = mask.bit_count()
if size <= max_size:
continue
# collect selected points
sel = [points[i] for i in range(N) if (mask>>i)&1]
# check condition
ok = True
# group by rows and columns
rows = {}
cols = {}
for (r,c) in sel:
rows.setdefault(r, []).append(c)
cols.setdefault(c, []).append(r)
# for each pair of rows
for r1, r2 in itertools.combinations(rows.keys(), 2):
cols1 = rows[r1]
cols2 = rows[r2]
if len(cols1) < 2 or len(cols2) < 2:
    continue
# check all pairs of two points in each row
for a,b in itertools.combinations(cols1,2):
    for c,d in itertools.combinations(cols2,2):
        if abs(a - c) == abs(b - d) or (a + b) == (c + d):
            ok = False
            break
    if not ok:
        break
if not ok:
    break
if not ok:
continue
# vertical condition
for c1, c2 in itertools.combinations(cols.keys(), 2):
rows1 = cols[c1]
rows2 = cols[c2]
if len(rows1) < 2 or len(rows2) < 2:
    continue
for a,b in itertools.combinations(rows1,2):
    for c,d in itertools.combinations(rows2,2):
        if abs(a - c) == abs(b - d) or (a + b) == (c + d):
            ok = False
            break
    if not ok:
        break
if not ok:
    break
if ok:
max_size = size
best = sel
    return max_size, best

for n in range(2,5):
    maxsize, best = max_subset_no_iso_trapezoid(n)
    print(n, maxsize)

We need to determine if such permutations exist for any odd n? Let's analyze.

Observation: The condition that adjacent sums are distinct is equivalent to requiring that the pairs (p_i, p_{i+1}) have distinct unordered sums. Since sums are p_i + p_{i+1}, they are distinct if the unordered pairs are distinct (they are anyway) and sums differ.

Given that differences are distinct, we have a permutation where each adjacent pair is unique in both sum and difference.

This is reminiscent of constructing a "Skolem sequence" or "Langford pairing"? Not exactly.

Maybe we can use the permutation p_i = i (identity). Then differences are all 1, not distinct.

We need a permutation where each adjacent pair (a,b) yields a unique difference and sum. Since there are n-1 pairs, we need n-1 distinct differences (1..n-1) and n-1 distinct sums (range 3..2n-1). So we need a bijection between differences and sums perhaps.

We can try to construct using the following: Let p_i = i for i odd, and p_i = n+1 - i for i even? Not sure.

Let's attempt constructive approach: Use the "alternating high-low" permutation for differences, but modify to make sums distinct. The issue with sums repeating arises because pattern 1, n, 2, n-1,... yields sums: 1+n, n+2, 2+(n-1), (n-1)+3, ... which alternate between n+1, n+2, n+1, n+2,... So repeats.

We need to break that pattern.

Let's try to design for general n: Let’s pair numbers to produce distinct sums: we can aim for sums to be all distinct and maybe monotonic (strictly increasing). Since each sum is sum of two consecutive numbers, we can try to make the sequence of sums strictly increasing. That would guarantee distinctness.

If we can arrange p_i such that p_i + p_{i+1} is increasing, then sums are distinct. Also we need differences distinct.

Is it possible to have both monotonic increasing sums and distinct differences? Let's think.

Suppose we have p_i < p_{i+1} for all i (strictly increasing). Then sums are increasing, differences are positive but not necessarily distinct; they could repeat. For example, identity permutation gives differences all 1 (repeat). To make differences distinct, we need differences to be distinct positive integers. So we need a strictly increasing sequence where successive differences are distinct. That's just any strictly increasing sequence of distinct numbers where the gaps are distinct. That's possible: e.g., p_i = i(i+1)/2? But must be a permutation of 1..n, not arbitrary numbers.

Thus we need a permutation where the gaps (differences) are a permutation of 1..n-1, and the sums are increasing. Since sums are p_i + p_{i+1} = p_i + (p_i + d_i) = 2p_i + d_i, where d_i = p_{i+1} - p_i (positive). Since d_i are distinct, we need to choose p_i such that 2p_i + d_i is increasing.

Given that p_i are numbers from 1..n, we can attempt to construct recursively.

Alternatively, we can think of constructing a Hamiltonian path in the complete bipartite graph where the column permutation is the same as row permutation but we can also shift.

But maybe we don't need to satisfy sum distinctness across rows because the condition a+b = c+d may be automatically avoided if differences are distinct? Let's examine: For two rows with intervals (a,b) and (c,d), if a+b = c+d, then the intervals are symmetric about same midpoint. Could this happen even if differences are distinct? Yes, e.g., intervals (1,4) (difference 3) and (2,3) (difference 1) have sums 5 each, but differences distinct. So we need to avoid equal sums as well.

Thus we need both constraints.

Now, perhaps the maximum number of points is less than 2n-1 due to sum condition. Let's attempt to compute upper bound using both constraints.

We have sum over rows of C(a_y,2) ≤ n-1 (differences). Similarly sum over rows of C(a_y,2) ≤ n-1 (sums). Actually both constraints give same bound, but they are not independent: each row with a_y=2 contributes 1 to each sum of differences and sums. So each row with 2 points consumes one difference value and one sum value. Since there are only n-1 possible differences and 2n-3 possible sums, the sum constraint is not limiting (since we have at most n-1 rows with 2 points, we need n-1 distinct sums, which is possible because there are 2n-3 > n-1). So the sum constraint does not reduce the bound; it's just that we need to assign distinct sums, which is possible.

Thus the upper bound remains 2n-1.

The sums of consecutive vertex labels correspond to something else. In graceful labeling, vertex labels are distinct but not necessarily produce distinct sums. Let's analyze the sum condition more carefully. For rows, we need that for any two rows with intervals (a,b) and (c,d), we cannot have a+b = c+d. This is equivalent to forbidding two intervals with same midpoint. So we need to avoid having two rows with intervals symmetric about same vertical line.

If we assign each row a unique midpoint (i.e., (a+b)/2 distinct), that's equivalent to distinct sums (since sum = 2*midpoint). So we need distinct midpoints across rows.

Thus we need to assign to each row a unique midpoint (which can be half-integer). For rows with a single point (degree 1), there is no interval, so no constraint.

Thus we need to assign to each row with degree 2 a unique midpoint value (which can be integer or half-integer). Since there are n-1 rows with degree 2, we need n-1 distinct midpoints. The possible midpoints are of the form (i+j)/2 where i,j ∈ [1,n] distinct. The number of possible midpoints is about 2n-1 (including half-integers). So we have enough.

Thus we need to construct a Hamiltonian path where each row's adjacent columns have distinct midpoints and distinct differences.

Similarly for columns.

But perhaps there is a theorem: For n>3, there is no permutation of [1..n] with both distinct adjacent differences and distinct adjacent sums. Let's attempt to prove.

Suppose we have permutation p_1,...,p_n. Consider the set of sums s_i = p_i + p_{i+1}. Since p_i are distinct, the sums are all distinct if and only if the multiset of unordered pairs {p_i,p_{i+1}} have distinct sums. Since there are n-1 sums, each sum is between 3 and 2n-1. There are 2n-3 possible sums, so it's possible.

But maybe parity constraints cause repeats when differences are all distinct. Let's analyze.

Let d_i = p_{i+1} - p_i (could be positive or negative). Since differences are distinct in absolute value, the set {|d_i|} = {1,...,n-1}. Also the signs of d_i can be positive or negative.

Now sum s_i = p_i + p_{i+1} = 2p_i + d_i = 2p_{i+1} - d_i.

If we have two indices i<j with s_i = s_j, then 2p_i + d_i = 2p_j + d_j => 2(p_i - p_j) = d_j - d_i. Since RHS magnitude ≤ (n-1) - 1 = n-2? Actually d_j and d_i are distinct integers in [- (n-1), n-1] but not zero. Their difference could be up to n-2. LHS is even integer multiple of 2. So we need d_j - d_i to be even. That's possible.

But maybe we can argue that with distinct absolute differences covering all values, sums must repeat due to pigeonhole principle? Let's count parity.

Observe that for each i, s_i mod 2 = (p_i + p_{i+1}) mod 2 = (p_i + p_i + d_i) mod 2 = (2p_i + d_i) mod 2 = d_i mod 2. So parity of sum equals parity of difference (since 2p_i even). Thus s_i parity = d_i parity.

Since the set of absolute differences includes both even and odd numbers. For n≥4, there are at least two even differences (e.g., 2,4) and at least two odd differences (1,3). So the parity pattern of sums matches parity of differences. Since differences are all distinct, parity repeats according to number of even/odd differences. For n=5, differences {1,2,3,4} have parity pattern: odd, even, odd, even. So sums parity pattern is same: odd, even, odd, even. So sums could be distinct even if parity repeats, because there are multiple odd sums possible.

But maybe there is a stronger constraint: Since sums are p_i + p_{i+1} = p_i + p_i + d_i = 2p_i + d_i, and p_i are distinct, maybe we can show that if all |d_i| are distinct, then sums cannot all be distinct for n≥4.

Let's attempt to prove by contradiction: Suppose we have permutation p with distinct absolute differences. Consider the set of values p_i. Since differences cover all values 1..n-1, the sum of absolute differences is sum_{k=1}^{n-1} k = n(n-1)/2 = total variation of the permutation (sum of absolute steps). This equals sum_{i=1}^{n-1} |p_{i+1} - p_i| = total distance traveled. This is at most (n-1)*(n-1) maybe? Not helpful.

Maybe we can use the fact that sums s_i = p_i + p_{i+1} are all distinct, but there are only n-1 sums and 2n-3 possible values. So there is slack.

Thus existence may be possible for larger n.

Let total points N. Let r2 be number of rows with degree 2, r1 rows with degree 1, etc. Similarly columns.

We have sum_i a_i = N, sum_i C(a_i,2) = r2 (since each row with degree 2 contributes 1). So r2 ≤ n-1.

Similarly columns: c2 ≤ n-1.

Now N = r2*2 + r1*1 + ... but rows with degree >2 not allowed because they'd increase C(a_i,2) beyond 1 per row, reducing total N.

Thus to maximize N, we want as many rows as possible with degree 2, i.e., r2 = n-1 (max). Then remaining row(s) have degree 1 (since total rows = n). So we have r1 = 1. So N = 2*(n-1) + 1 = 2n-1.

Similarly columns: c2 = n-1, c1 = 1.

Thus the degree sequences are forced as earlier.

Let permutation p1..p4.

We need differences set {1,2,3}. Let's consider possible patterns.

Case 1: Differences order (3,2,1). Then we have p2 = p1 ±3, p3 = p2 ±2, p4 = p3 ±1. Since numbers are 1..4, we can try.

p1=3 => p2 = 0 or 6 out.

p1=4 => p2 = 1 (since -3). Then p3 = p2 ±2 = 3 or -1 => 3. Then p4 = p3 ±1 = 2 or 4 (4 used). So p4 =2. Permutation: 4,1,3,2. Sums:5,4,5 repeat. Fails.

p1=4 => p2=1. Then p3 = p2 ±1 = 2 or 0 => 2. Then p4 = p3 ±2 = 0 or 4 (4 used) => 0 out. Fail.

import itertools

def exists_config(N):
    # try to find a selection of points with at least two rows each having >=2 points, but no common midpoint across rows
    # also need to avoid column condition? For now just test row condition.
    points = [(x,y) for x in range(1,N+1) for y in range(1,N+1)]
    # brute force all subsets? Too many. Instead search for small N by constructing rows manually.
    # We'll try to assign sets of x per row.
    rows = {}
    # choose two rows y1,y2
    for y1 in range(1,N+1):
        for y2 in range(y1+1,N+1):
            # choose subsets A,B each size >=2
            xs = list(range(1,N+1))
            for sizeA in range(2,N+1):
                for A in itertools.combinations(xs,sizeA):
                    midsA = set((a+b)/2 for a,b in itertools.combinations(A,2))
                    for sizeB in range(2,N+1):
                        for B in itertools.combinations(xs,sizeB):
                            midsB = set((c+d)/2 for c,d in itertools.combinations(B,2))
                            if midsA.isdisjoint(midsB):
                                # found rows with no common midpoint
                                return (y1,A,y2,B)
    return None

for N in range(2,8):
    res = exists_config(N)
    print(N, res is not None)

import itertools

def exists_config(N):
    # try to find a selection of points with at least two rows each having >=2 points, but no common midpoint across rows
    # also need to avoid column condition? For now just test row condition.
    points = [(x,y) for x in range(1,N+1) for y in range(1,N+1)]
    # brute force all subsets? Too many. Instead search for small N by constructing rows manually.
    # We'll try to assign sets of x per row.
    rows = {}
    # choose two rows y1,y2
    for y1 in range(1,N+1):
        for y2 in range(y1+1,N+1):
            # choose subsets A,B each size >=2
            xs = list(range(1,N+1))
            for sizeA in range(2,N+1):
                for A in itertools.combinations(xs,sizeA):
                    midsA = set((a+b)/2 for a,b in itertools.combinations(A,2))
                    for sizeB in range(2,N+1):
                        for B in itertools.combinations(xs,sizeB):
                            midsB = set((c+d)/2 for c,d in itertools.combinations(B,2))
                            if midsA.isdisjoint(midsB):
                                # found rows with no common midpoint
                                return (y1,A,y2,B)
    return None

for N in range(2,8):
    res = exists_config(N)
    print(N, res is not None)

            for sizeA in range(2,N+1):

                        for B in itertools.combinations(xs,sizeB):
                            midsB = set((c+d)/2 for c,d in itertools.combinations(B,2))

[CODE] (turn 4):
from itertools import combinations, product
import numpy as np, math, itertools, sys, time, random, collections, math, itertools, sys, time
from scipy.optimize import milp, LinearConstraint, Bounds

def max_points(N):
    # variables: N*N binary
    n_vars = N*N
    # objective: maximize sum of variables
    c = -np.ones(n_vars)  # milp minimizes, so negative
    # constraints: for each forbidden quadruple, sum <= 3
    # generate all quadruples that form isosceles trapezoid with horizontal or vertical bases
    rows = range(N)
    cols = range(N)
    # map (x,y) to index
    def idx(x,y):
        return y*N + x  # x from 0..N-1, y from 0..N-1
    constraints = []
    # horizontal bases
    for y1 in rows:
        for y2 in rows:
            if y1 >= y2: continue
            for x1 in cols:
                for x2 in cols:
                    if x1 >= x2: continue
                    # base1: (x1,y1)-(x2,y1)
                    # need another base at y2 with same midpoint
                    # find x3,x4 such that (x3+x4)/2 = (x1+x2)/2 and x3<x4
                    # So x3+x4 = x1+x2
                    s = x1 + x2
                    # choose unordered pair (x3,x4) distinct, with sum s, within range
                    for x3 in cols:
                        x4 = s - x3
                        if x4 <= x3: continue
                        if 0 <= x4 < N:
                            # points: (x1,y1),(x2,y1),(x3,y2),(x4,y2)
                            quad = [idx(x1,y1), idx(x2,y1), idx(x3,y2), idx(x4,y2)]
                            # add constraint sum <=3
                            row = np.zeros(n_vars)
                            row[quad] = 1
                            constraints.append(row)
    # vertical bases (similar)
    for x1 in cols:
        for x2 in cols:
            if x1 >= x2: continue
            for y1 in rows:
                for y2 in rows:
                    if y1 >= y2: continue
                    s = y1 + y2
                    for y3 in rows:
                        y4 = s - y3
                        if y4 <= y3: continue
                        if 0 <= y4 < N:
                            quad = [idx(x1,y1), idx(x1,y2), idx(x2,y3), idx(x2,y4)]
                            row = np.zeros(n_vars)
                            row[quad] = 1
                            constraints.append(row)
    # Build A matrix
    A = np.vstack(constraints)
    # each constraint: sum <=3
    # Convert to LinearConstraint with ub=3, lb=-inf
    lc = LinearConstraint(A, -np.inf*np.ones(A.shape[0]), 3*np.ones(A.shape[0]))
    bounds = Bounds(0,1)
    res = milp(c, constraints=lc, bounds=bounds, integrality=np.ones(n_vars))
    if not res.success:
        print("MILP failed")
        return None
    val = -res.fun
    # round
    return int(round(val))

for N in range(2,6):
    print(N, max_points(N))

from itertools import combinations, product

from scipy.optimize import milp, LinearConstraint, Bounds

def max_points(N):
    # variables: N*N binary
    n_vars = N*N
    # objective: maximize sum of variables
    c = -np.ones(n_vars)  # milp minimizes, so negative
    # constraints: for each forbidden quadruple, sum <= 3
    # generate all quadruples that form isosceles trapezoid with horizontal or vertical bases
    rows = range(N)
    cols = range(N)
    # map (x,y) to index
    def idx(x,y):
        return y*N + x  # x from 0..N-1, y from 0..N-1
    constraints = []
    # horizontal bases
    for y1 in rows:
        for y2 in rows:
            if y1 >= y2: continue
            for x1 in cols:
                for x2 in cols:
                    if x1 >= x2: continue
                    # base1: (x1,y1)-(x2,y1)
                    # need another base at y2 with same midpoint
                    # find x3,x4 such that (x3+x4)/2 = (x1+x2)/2 and x3<x4
                    # So x3+x4 = x1+x2
                    s = x1 + x2
                    # choose unordered pair (x3,x4) distinct, with sum s, within range
                    for x3 in cols:
                        x4 = s - x3
                        if x4 <= x3: continue
                        if 0 <= x4 < N:
                            # points: (x1,y1),(x2,y1),(x3,y2),(x4,y2)
                            quad = [idx(x1,y1), idx(x2,y1), idx(x3,y2), idx(x4,y2)]
                            # add constraint sum <=3
                            row = np.zeros(n_vars)
                            row[quad] = 1
                            constraints.append(row)
    # vertical bases (similar)
    for x1 in cols:
        for x2 in cols:
            if x1 >= x2: continue
            for y1 in rows:
                for y2 in rows:
                    if y1 >= y2: continue
                    s = y1 + y2
                    for y3 in rows:
                        y4 = s - y3
                        if y4 <= y3: continue
                        if 0 <= y4 < N:
                            quad = [idx(x1,y1), idx(x1,y2), idx(x2,y3), idx(x2,y4)]
                            row = np.zeros(n_vars)
                            row[quad] = 1
                            constraints.append(row)
    # Build A matrix
    A = np.vstack(constraints)
    # each constraint: sum <=3
    # Convert to LinearConstraint with ub=3, lb=-inf

    bounds = Bounds(0,1)
    res = milp(c, constraints=lc, bounds=bounds, integrality=np.ones(n_vars))
    if not res.success:
        print("MILP failed")
        return None
    val = -res.fun
    # round
    return int(round(val))

def get_solution(N):
    n_vars = N*N
    def idx(x,y):
        return y*N + x
    c = -np.ones(n_vars)
    constraints = []
    # generate constraints as before
    rows = range(N)
    cols = range(N)
    for y1 in rows:
        for y2 in rows:
            if y1 >= y2: continue
            for x1 in cols:
                for x2 in cols:
                    if x1 >= x2: continue
                    s = x1 + x2
                    for x3 in cols:
                        x4 = s - x3
                        if x4 <= x3: continue
                        if 0 <= x4 < N:
                            quad = [idx(x1,y1), idx(x2,y1), idx(x3,y2), idx(x4,y2)]
                            row = np.zeros(n_vars)
                            row[quad] = 1
                            constraints.append(row)
    for x1 in cols:
        for x2 in cols:
            if x1 >= x2: continue
            for y1 in rows:
                for y2 in rows:
                    if y1 >= y2: continue
                    s = y1 + y2
                    for y3 in rows:
                        y4 = s - y3
                        if y4 <= y3: continue
                        if 0 <= y4 < N:
                            quad = [idx(x1,y1), idx(x1,y2), idx(x2,y3), idx(x2,y4)]
                            row = np.zeros(n_vars)
                            row[quad] = 1
                            constraints.append(row)
    A = np.vstack(constraints)
    lc = LinearConstraint(A, -np.inf*np.ones(A.shape[0]), 3*np.ones(A.shape[0]))
    bounds = Bounds(0,1)
    res = milp(c, constraints=lc, bounds=bounds, integrality=np.ones(n_vars))
    if not res.success:
        print("fail")
        return None
    sol = (res.x > 0.5).astype(int)
    points = [(i%N, i//N) for i in range(n_vars) if sol[i]==1]
    return points

        for x2 in cols:
            if x1 >= x2: continue
            for y1 in rows:
                for y2 in rows:
                    if y1 >= y2: continue
                    s = y1 + y2
                    for y3 in rows:
                        y4 = s - y3
                        if y4 <= y3: continue
                        if 0 <= y4 < N:
                            quad = [idx(x1,y1), idx(x1,y2), idx(x2,y3), idx(x2,y4)]
                            row = np.zeros(n_vars)
                            row[quad] = 1
                            constraints.append(row)
    A = np.vstack(constraints)
    lc = LinearConstraint(A, -np.inf*np.ones(A.shape[0]), 3*np.ones(A.shape[0]))
    bounds = Bounds(0,1)
    res = milp(c, constraints=lc, bounds=bounds, integrality=np.ones(n_vars))
    if not res.success:
        print("fail")
        return None
    sol = (res.x > 0.5).astype(int)
    points = [(i%N, i//N) for i in range(n_vars) if sol[i]==1]
    return points

pts5 = get_solution(5)
len(pts5), pts5[:20]

from networkx.algorithms import bipartite
def construct(N, t):
    # degree sequences
    rows_deg = [3]*t + [2]*(N-t)
    cols_deg = [3]*t + [2]*(N-t)
    # sort descending
    rows_deg.sort(reverse=True)
    cols_deg.sort(reverse=True)
    G = bipartite.havel_hakimi_graph(rows_deg, cols_deg)
    return G

G = construct(5,2)
print(G.number_of_edges())
print([d for n,d in G.degree()][:5])

[CODE] (turn 16):
from networkx.algorithms import bipartite
def construct(N, t):
    # degree sequences
    rows_deg = [3]*t + [2]*(N-t)
    cols_deg = [3]*t + [2]*(N-t)
    # sort descending
    rows_deg.sort(reverse=True)
    cols_deg.sort(reverse=True)
    G = bipartite.havel_hakimi_graph(rows_deg, cols_deg)
    return G

G = construct(5,2)
print(G.number_of_edges())
print([d for n,d in G.degree()][:5])

[CODE] (turn 17):
def max_selection(N, target):
    rows = [set() for _ in range(N)]
    cols = [set() for _ in range(N)]
    row_sum_used = {}  # sum -> row index
    col_sum_used = {}
    best = 0
    solution = None
    
    # order points maybe by some heuristic; we can try recursion over cells
    cells = [(x,y) for y in range(N) for x in range(N)]
    
    # sort maybe by degree potential? Not needed.
    
    # Use simple backtracking with pruning
    # Use recursion index i over cells
    sys.setrecursionlimit(10000)
    def backtrack(i, count):
        nonlocal best, solution
        # pruning: if count + (len(cells)-i) <= best: return
        if count + (len(cells)-i) <= best:
            return
        if i == len(cells):
            if count > best:
                best = count
                # store solution
                solution = [(x,y) for y in range(N) for x in range(N) if (x,y) in selected]
            return
        x,y = cells[i]
        # option 1: skip
        backtrack(i+1, count)
        # option 2: try to select point (x,y)
        # check constraints
        # compute new row sums
        conflict = False
        new_row_sums = []
        for x2 in rows[y]:
            s = x + x2
            owner = row_sum_used.get(s)
            if owner is not None and owner != y:
                conflict = True
                break
            new_row_sums.append(s)
        if conflict:
            return
        # compute new column sums
        new_col_sums = []
        for y2 in cols[x]:
            s = y + y2
            owner = col_sum_used.get(s)
            if owner is not None and owner != x:
                conflict = True
                break
            new_col_sums.append(s)
        if conflict:
            return
        # add point
        rows[y].add(x)
        cols[x].add(y)
        # record previous owners to restore
        saved_row = {}
        for s in new_row_sums:
            saved_row[s] = row_sum_used.get(s)
            row_sum_used[s] = y
        saved_col = {}
        for s in new_col_sums:
            saved_col[s] = col_sum_used.get(s)
            col_sum_used[s] = x
        selected.add((x,y))
        backtrack(i+1, count+1)
        # backtrack removal
        selected.remove((x,y))
        rows[y].remove(x)
        cols[x].remove(y)
        for s, prev in saved_row.items():
            if prev is None:
                del row_sum_used[s]
            else:
                row_sum_used[s] = prev
        for s, prev in saved_col.items():
            if prev is None:
                del col_sum_used[s]
            else:
                col_sum_used[s] = prev
    
    selected = set()
    backtrack(0,0)
    return best, solution

best5, sol5 = max_selection(5, 12)
print("best5", best5)
